```
; fn: the POST transaction machine (Builder P; RUNTIME-MODEL section 3 as ruled
; 2026-10-10: PAYLOAD_WRITING -> LOG_WRITING -> COMMITTING -> PUBLISHED, no payload
; barrier).  Packet draft 3: the start is a continuation (the head is read and
; accepted in quantum-sized windows on the zero-delay timer), the article is written
; from the poster's buffer unmoved, and the step first checks the state's shape
; against bounded per-field budgets, so the layer's obligations (charged, bounded
; requests and state) hold of every state.  The keystones P0-P6 are stated in the
; packet (build/post-runs/, the lane's scratch) and not yet proved, so this book is
; not in ACL2_BOOKS and certifies nothing of record.
;
; The owner, static slot 1 under contract v2.1, takes a poster's handed article
; buffer and writes one log entry from three spans: b1 (frame header, chain field,
; the record's prefix PRE) from an owner workspace, the article [0, len) from the
; poster's buffer unmoved, b3 (the record's suffix SUF and the trailer) from a
; second workspace; the record is PRE ++ article ++ SUF, the header edits being the
; block PRE carries before the head.  The padding is never written (the extent past
; the frontier reads zeros).  The three writes are L's fn-ptd one record write
; (books/post-transaction-durable.lisp); :posted only on the barrier's (:done).  The
; digest is BLAKE3 over 1024-octet windows on the zero-delay timer
; (books/blake3-tree.lisp's held state).  Geometry (unit fmax hmax) is the opened
; store's, never a constant (D27).
(in-package "ACL2")
(include-book "post-transaction-durable")
(include-book "runtime-contract-layer")
(include-book "blake3-tree")
(set-verify-guards-eagerness 0)

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; Bounds and constants: properties of this code, none bounds data (D27).
(defconst *fn-ptx-k* 2)              ; posts waiting, at most
(defconst *fn-ptx-lfile* 2)          ; the log segment's file reference
(defconst *fn-ptx-wexp* 0)           ; digest window = 1024 * 2^wexp octets
(defconst *fn-ptx-window* 1024)
(defconst *fn-ptx-nw-max* 64)        ; digest windows per step at most (a quantum cap)
(defconst *fn-ptx-sufhead-max* 9)    ; the suffix's head (a CBOR uint), at most; b3's base
(defconst *fn-ptx-codes* '((:posted . 0) (:refused . 1) (:uncertain . 2) (:busy . 3)))
(defun fn-ptx-code (o) (nfix (cdr (assoc-eq o *fn-ptx-codes*))))

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; The acceptance, constrained and streaming (the seam with M's admission gate,
; settled 10-10 word for word): begun at the record's txid, fed the head's octets
; window by window, each feed answering the next state and a part of the record's
; suffix; ended with the article body's length, answering whether the article is
; accepted, the record's prefix PRE (the record fields before the article, the
; article's byte-string header, the injected header block), the Path insert INS and
; its offset IOFF in the article (D32: the agent is spliced in at the Path content),
; the suffix's head SUFHEAD (the group count, known only at the end) and its tail.
; record = PRE ++ article[0,IOFF) ++ INS ++ article[IOFF,len) ++ SUFHEAD ++ the
; parts ++ the tail.  The state and the outputs are bounded by constants of the
; gate's code: K = (fn-ptx-acc-out-max), M's (fn-adm-acc-out-max); SUFHEAD by the
; widest CBOR uint head; the state by the codec's group-name ceiling and naturals.
(encapsulate
  (((fn-ptx-acc-begin *) => *)
   ((fn-ptx-acc-feed * *) => (mv * *))
   ((fn-ptx-acc-feed-cost * *) => *)
   ((fn-ptx-acc-end * *) => (mv * * * * * *))
   ((fn-ptx-acc-max-state) => *)
   ((fn-ptx-acc-out-max) => *))
  (local (defun fn-ptx-acc-begin (txid) (declare (ignore txid)) nil))
  (local (defun fn-ptx-acc-feed (a octets) (declare (ignore a octets)) (mv nil nil)))
  (local (defun fn-ptx-acc-feed-cost (a octets) (declare (ignore a octets)) 0))
  (local (defun fn-ptx-acc-end (a blen) (declare (ignore a blen)) (mv t nil nil 0 nil nil)))
  (local (defun fn-ptx-acc-max-state () 1))
  (local (defun fn-ptx-acc-out-max () 0))
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-max-state-natp (natp (fn-ptx-acc-max-state)) :rule-classes :type-prescription)
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-out-max-natp (natp (fn-ptx-acc-out-max)) :rule-classes :type-prescription)
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-begin-bounded (<= (fn-rtc-size (fn-ptx-acc-begin txid)) (fn-ptx-acc-max-state))
    :rule-classes :linear)
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-feed-bounded (<= (fn-rtc-size (mv-nth 0 (fn-ptx-acc-feed a octets))) (fn-ptx-acc-max-state))
    :rule-classes :linear)
  ; the feed's work (the instance's def-cost bound): linear in the octets fed
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-feed-cost-bounded
    (and (natp (fn-ptx-acc-feed-cost a octets))
         (<= (fn-ptx-acc-feed-cost a octets) (+ (* 4 (len octets)) (fn-ptx-acc-out-max))))
    :rule-classes nil)
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-feed-shape
    (and (fn-cbor-octet-listp (mv-nth 1 (fn-ptx-acc-feed a octets)))
         (<= (len (mv-nth 1 (fn-ptx-acc-feed a octets))) (+ (* 2 (len octets)) (fn-ptx-acc-out-max))))
    :rule-classes nil)
  (defthm fn-ptx-acc-end-shape
    (mv-let (ok pre ins ioff sufhead suf) (fn-ptx-acc-end a blen)
      (declare (ignore ok))
      (and (fn-cbor-octet-listp pre) (fn-cbor-octet-listp ins) (natp ioff)
           (fn-cbor-octet-listp sufhead) (fn-cbor-octet-listp suf)
           (<= (len pre) (fn-ptx-acc-out-max)) (<= (len ins) (fn-ptx-acc-out-max))
           (<= (len sufhead) *fn-ptx-sufhead-max*) (<= (len suf) (fn-ptx-acc-out-max))))
    :rule-classes nil))

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; State: (ph k cur q kd ext nxt b1 b3 hs committed evd geo acc).
;   cur  (fid finc bp len hoff txid ioff): the poster's slot, its handed buffer BP
;        holding the article at [0,len); HOFF the head's length once found (while
;        :reading, the position the head search has reached); the record's txid; IOFF
;        the Path insert's offset in the article (len when there is no insert)
;   q    waiting posts (fid finc bp len), at most *fn-ptx-k*
;   kd   the log kernel's projection (count last frontier next-txid acked): what
;        fn-ptd's actions read of fn-lgk, without COMMITTED's elements
;   ext nxt  fn-ptd's extent and the sealed extent
;   b1   owner workspace (h g l1 lins): [0,l1) = frame header ++ chain field ++ PRE,
;        [l1, l1+lins) = INS
;   b3   owner workspace (h g end start): [start,end) = SUF, then the trailer once the
;        digest is known (end then past it); while :reading, start is the base
;        *fn-ptx-sufhead-max* and end the suffix parts' running end
;   hs   the digest's held state (pos stack); after the last window (nmsg trailer pad)
;   evd  ghost: this transaction's completions (kind tag n), in order
;   geo  (unit fmax hmax), the opened store's
;   acc  the acceptance's state while :reading
(defun fn-ptx-mk (ph k cur q kd ext nxt b1 b3 hs committed evd geo acc)
  (list ph (nfix k) cur q kd (nfix ext) (nfix nxt) b1 b3 hs (and committed t) evd geo acc))
(defun fn-ptx-ph (m) (fn-rtc-get 0 m))
(defun fn-ptx-k (m) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 1 m)))
(defun fn-ptx-cur (m) (fn-rtc-get 2 m))
(defun fn-ptx-q (m) (fn-rtc-get 3 m))
(defun fn-ptx-kd (m) (fn-rtc-get 4 m))
(defun fn-ptx-ext (m) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 5 m)))
(defun fn-ptx-nxt (m) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 6 m)))
(defun fn-ptx-b1 (m) (fn-rtc-get 7 m))
(defun fn-ptx-b3 (m) (fn-rtc-get 8 m))
(defun fn-ptx-hs (m) (fn-rtc-get 9 m))
(defun fn-ptx-committedp (m) (and (fn-rtc-get 10 m) t))
```

; The POST transaction machine as a runtime-contract layer instance (Builder P, landing 1).
;
; fn-rtc-def-layer instantiates the contract (v2.2) with the machine fn-ptx-step over the pool
; stobj: every slot's machine is fn-ptx, a static slot starts :closed (fn-ptx-closed) and opens on
; the :opened completion fn-rtc-init arms for it, and a connection slot holds a :closed machine
; that refuses every post.  The instance's keystones (fn-ptl-<keystone>, T1-T15) and its exec
; equality fn-ptl-mx-step-is-m-step (the stobj step is the logic step over the slot's view) come
; from the generator, and the store-opened facts KO1-KO3 of contract v2.2 (not in the generator's
; list) by the same functional instance below.  The machine's obligations are the machine book's
; fn-ptx-step-facts, fn-ptx-step-reads-view and fn-ptx-commits-only-on-fsync-done.
(in-package "ACL2")
(include-book "post-transaction")
(include-book "runtime-contract-opened")
; The obligations are discharged from the machine's lemmas by :use, in the machine's theory as of
; its P5 statement (its later proof lemmas loop the rewriter on the generator's goals).
(local (in-theory (current-theory 'fn-ptx-p5)))
; The machine's guards are not verified (its book runs at eagerness 0), so the instance's exec
; copies are admitted without guard verification too; fn-ptx-step's guards are landing 2's, where
; the host first executes it.
(set-verify-guards-eagerness 0)

(defun fn-ptx-static-init (j) (declare (ignore j)) (fn-ptx-closed))

; The layer's invariant runs (fn-ptl-mstates-okp, fn-ptl-invp) and reads these bounds.
(verify-guards fn-ptx-max-state)
(verify-guards fn-ptx-committedp)
(verify-guards fn-ptx-max-reqs)
(verify-guards fn-ptx-wf-cost)
(verify-guards fn-ptx-c)

(defthm fn-ptx-closed-facts
  (and (equal (fn-rtc-size (fn-ptx-closed)) 29)
       (not (fn-ptx-committedp (fn-ptx-closed))))
  :rule-classes nil)

(fn-rtc-def-layer fn-ptl
  :step fn-ptx-step :init fn-ptx-closed :static-init fn-ptx-static-init
  :committedp fn-ptx-committedp :c fn-ptx-c :max-reqs fn-ptx-max-reqs :max-state fn-ptx-max-state
  :reads-view fn-ptx-step-reads-view

                 fn-ptx-closed-facts))))

; fn-rtc-init-emits-the-opened, KO1b fn-rtc-opened-use-is-static, KO2 fn-rtc-opened-acts-at-most-once,
; KO2r fn-rtc-machine-cannot-request-the-opened, KO1c fn-rtc-opened-acts-once-per-incarnation, KO3

; arrives once, at its static slot, and steps exactly that slot's machine.
;; KO1c quantifies over runs (fn-rtc-run), which the generator does not copy: the instance's run.
(defun fn-ptl-run (s events q)
  (if (consp events)
      (mv-let (s2 acts) (fn-ptl-step s (car events) q)
        (declare (ignore acts))
        (fn-ptl-run s2 (cdr events) q))
    s))

;;; The runtime contract's multi-instance exercise (RUNTIME-MODEL section 9
;;; item 1), developer images only:
;;;
;;;   fn rtc-exercise run [VARIANT]
;;;
;;; The script is the book's (books/runtime-contract-echo.lisp
;;; `fn-rce-exercise'): host completions over two interleaved connections,
;;; which completions the host delivers and which input it lands first;
;;; The book supplies the seven-field configuration and reserves three fixed
;;; buffers per connection. VARIANT 0 is the script; 1-4 inject a host fault the
;;; checks must report.  The run is the extracted
;;; executable layer `fn-rcl-x-step*' over the abstract stobj `fn-rtc-st',
;;; the one array-backed pool; one line per step, the step's observation:
;;; (i kind actions refused invariant stability match).  The exit is ok when
;;; every step kept the invariant and the outstanding :out octets and no
;;; unmatched completion changed the state; else fault.  The host only calls
;;; the book through `fnn-call' and prints: the script, the landing, the step
;;; and every check are the book's.
(in-package "ACL2")

(defvar *fnn-rtc-st* nil)

(defun fnn-live-rtc-st ()
  (or *fnn-rtc-st*
      (setq *fnn-rtc-st*
            (or (cdr (assoc 'fn-rtc-st (user-stobj-alist *the-live-state*)))
                (fnn-fault "the runtime contract stobj is not in this image")))))

(defun fnn-rtc-observation-sound-p (obs)
  (and (eq (nth 4 obs) :invp)
       (eq (nth 5 obs) :stable)
       (not (eq (nth 6 obs) :unmatched-changed))))

(defun fnn-command-rtc-exercise (command args)
  "Developer CLI: rtc-exercise run [VARIANT]."
  (unless (fnn-developer-image-p)
    (error 'fnn-usage-error
           :message "rtc-exercise is available only in the developer image"))
  (unless (and (string= command "run") (<= (length args) 1))
    (error 'fnn-usage-error :message "rtc-exercise run [VARIANT]"))
  (let* ((variant (if args
                      (handler-case (parse-integer (first args))
                        (error () (error 'fnn-usage-error :message "rtc-exercise VARIANT must be an integer")))
                    0))
         (result (fnn-call 'fn-rce-exercise variant (fnn-live-rtc-st)))
         (obs (second result)))
    (let ((*print-case* :downcase)
          (*print-pretty* nil)
          (*print-base* 10)
          (*print-radix* nil))
      (dolist (o obs)
        (fnn-out "~s" o)))
    (if (every #'fnn-rtc-observation-sound-p obs)
        +fnn-exit-ok+
      +fnn-exit-fault+)))

(fnn-register-verb "rtc-exercise" #'fnn-command-rtc-exercise)
582:; exercise over the live fn-rtc-st.
```

◀ result
```
bf5b06f60 host lease: the premises book, establishing the host path's premises of the executor's states
1dee89c43 host lease: HL5, the leased run sends exactly the render run's windows
e01e0325f host lease: teeth for the executor keystones
runtime-contract-host-lease-premises.lisp
runtime-contract-host-lease-run.lisp
runtime-contract-host-lease.cert
runtime-contract-host-lease.fasl
runtime-contract-host-lease.lisp
runtime-contract-host-lease.port
; fn: the host lease's executor (Builder A, the host lease; STATEMENTS-DRAFT's HOST EXECUTOR DESIGN).
;
; One ARTICLE response is one instance of the runtime contract over the process's one `fn-rtc-st':
;  begin    the host completes the listener's outstanding :accept with the connection's id as the
;           resource; the instance's machine (`fn-hlm-step') asks for a pool buffer on that :accept,
;           (:submit :pool nil nil), and the request waits in issue order; the host completes every
;           :grant and :pool action at once (`fn-hld-settle'), so when a pool buffer is free the
;           instance holds it as its workspace H when begin returns.
;  quantum  under the owner mutex the render leaves its window in `fn-dss-out' (adapter A2 until C's
;           window kind renders in place through fn-rtc-st-put); `fn-hld-quantum' copies it into H at
;           0, one fn-rtc-st-put per octet (no list, no vector), and submits (:submit :send (h g 0 n)):
;           exactly `fn-hl-quantum-state', so HL5a is the quantum's keystone.  The host writes the
;           array range [base(H), base(H)+n) of the stobj's one octet array, off the mutex, with no
;           copy (HL1b: no render touches H while the send is outstanding).
;  sent     the host completes the :send with the octets the socket took; H is the workspace again.
;  end      the host closes the instance; its close completion releases H to the pool, re-arms the
;           grant, and the next waiter is granted (settled at once).
; The machine is passive past the :accept: every other request is the host's, through the
; contract's own request functions, so every contract keystone about requests covers it.
(in-package "ACL2")
(include-book "runtime-contract-host-send")
(include-book "runtime-contract-host-pool")
(include-book "runtime-contract-layer")
(include-book "def-span-scan")

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; The leased reader's machine: on its :accept it asks for a pool buffer; it requests nothing else.

(defun fn-hlm-step (m ev fn-rtc-st q)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st) (ignore m q))
  (let ((ignore-st (fn-rtc-st-config fn-rtc-st)))
    (declare (ignore ignore-st))
    (if (eq (fn-rtc-get 0 ev) :accept)
        (mv nil (list (list :submit :pool nil nil)) 1)
      (mv nil nil 1))))

(defthm fn-hlm-step-reads-view
  (equal (fn-hlm-step m ev (fn-rtc-view-state (fn-rtc-ev-id ev) (fn-rtc-ev-inc ev) st) q) (fn-hlm-step m ev st q))
  :hints (("Goal" :in-theory (union-theories (theory 'minimal-theory)
                                             '(fn-hlm-step fn-rtc-v-readers-of-view-state)))))

(defthm fn-hlm-step-facts
  (and (equal (mv-nth 2 (fn-hlm-step m ev st q)) 1)
       (equal (fn-rtc-reqs-octets (mv-nth 1 (fn-hlm-step m ev st q))) 0)
       (<= (len (mv-nth 1 (fn-hlm-step m ev st q))) 1)
       (<= (fn-rtc-size (mv-nth 0 (fn-hlm-step m ev st q))) 1)))

(defun fn-hlm-init () (declare (xargs :guard t)) nil)
(defun fn-hlm-static-init (j) (declare (xargs :guard t) (ignore j)) nil)
(defun fn-hlm-committedp (m) (declare (xargs :guard t) (ignore m)) nil)
(defun fn-hlm-c () (declare (xargs :guard t)) 1)
(defun fn-hlm-max-reqs () (declare (xargs :guard t)) 1)
(defun fn-hlm-max-state () (declare (xargs :guard t)) 1)

(fn-rtc-def-layer fn-hll
  :step fn-hlm-step :init fn-hlm-init :static-init fn-hlm-static-init :committedp fn-hlm-committedp
  :c fn-hlm-c :max-reqs fn-hlm-max-reqs :max-state fn-hlm-max-state :reads-view fn-hlm-step-reads-view
  :hints (("Goal" :in-theory (enable fn-hll-m-step)
           :use ((:instance fn-hlm-step-facts (st pool))))))
```

▶ **Bash** `toolu_01JaJzffFaFur2TFJM1N1Ukj` — Read sim book header, rely, P4 defs
```
cd /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post; sed -n 1,32p books/post-transaction-sim.lisp; sed -n 95,236p books/post-transaction-sim.lisp
```

◀ result
```
; The POST transaction machine simulates L's commit driver (Builder P, landing 1: P4,
; AckEligible => DurableCommitEvidence for the machine).
;
; books/post-transaction.lisp is the machine (fn-ptx); books/post-transaction-durable.lisp is
; L's driver fn-ptd, whose keystone fn-ptd-posted-records-are-recovered-at-every-later-cut says
; every record fn-ptd answers :posted is recovered at every later cut.  This book maps the
; machine's completions to fn-ptd's inputs and relates the two states, so the records the machine
; answers :posted are fn-ptd's, and the keystone carries over; P6
; (fn-ptx-split-crash-images-are-entry-crash-images, books/post-transaction-crash.lisp) covers the
; cuts inside the machine's split entry write.
;
; The machine reads its message (the entry's octets, the spans of fn-ptx-spans) through the
; layer's view at each step; the environment's side is the rely (fn-ptx-rely): the octets the
; machine wrote at the head's end read back, and the message is unchanged from then on except for
; the trailer the last hashing step appends.  For layer runs the rely is NOT yet discharged (P4's
; open condition): ownership alone does not give it, since a refused layer write leaves the
; machine's advanced state in place; the discharge needs a joint invariant over the handed and
; workspace buffers, their generations, fill and capacity, and the accepted requests (landing 2).
; P4's composition is over the driver's run: that the machine's emitted writes are the split
; operations of P6 at the driver's offsets is landing 2's request/effect refinement.
(in-package "ACL2")
(include-book "post-transaction")
(include-book "post-transaction-crash")
(set-verify-guards-eagerness 0)
; The proofs below run in the machine's theory as of its P5 statement: the machine book's later
; proof lemmas (P0, P3) and the crash book's rules stay disabled here unless a hint names them.
(local (in-theory (current-theory 'fn-ptx-p5)))

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; The message and the record.

; The entry's head: the frame header (fixed length) and the chain field.
(defun fn-ptx-ptd-phase (ph)
  (case ph ((:idle :reading :hashing) :ready) (:extending :extending) (:extsyncing :extsyncing)
    ((:payload-writing :log-writing) :writing) (:committing :syncing) (:fenced :fenced) (otherwise nil)))
(defun fn-ptx-kd-before-take (ks)
  (fn-ptx-kd-mk (len (fn-lgk-committed ks)) (fn-lgk-last ks) (fn-lgk-frontier ks)
                (- (fn-lgk-next-txid ks) 1) (fn-lgk-acked ks)))
(defun fn-ptx-simp (m ptd id inc fn-rtc-st)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st))
  (let* ((ph (fn-ptx-ph m)) (pph (fn-ptd-ph ptd)) (ks (fn-ptd-ks ptd)))
    (and (equal pph (fn-ptx-ptd-phase ph))
         (case pph
           (:ready (and (equal (fn-ptx-kd m) (fn-ptx-kd-of ks))
                        (equal (fn-ptd-ext ptd) (fn-ptx-ext m))
                        (not (consp (fn-lgk-batch ks))) (not (consp (fn-lgk-inflight ks)))
                        (not (equal (fn-lgk-phase ks) :fault))
                        (equal (fn-lgk-acked ks) (len (fn-lgk-committed ks)))
                        (true-listp (fn-lgk-committed ks))

(defun-nx fn-ptx-trace-relies-p (m trace id inc)
  (declare (xargs :measure (len trace) :hints (("Goal" :in-theory (union-theories '(len o< o-finp o-p natp (:type-prescription len) (:compound-recognizer natp-compound-recognizer)) (theory 'minimal-theory))))))
  ; the rely at every step, and every event the machine's own slot's
  (if (atom trace) t
    (let* ((x (car trace)) (ev (nth 0 x)) (st (nth 1 x)) (q (nth 2 x))
           (st2 (if (consp (cdr trace)) (nth 1 (cadr trace)) st))
           (m2 (mv-nth 0 (fn-ptx-step m ev st q))))
      (and (equal (fn-rtc-ev-id ev) id) (equal (fn-rtc-ev-inc ev) inc)
           (fn-ptx-rely m m2 id inc st st2)
           (fn-ptx-trace-relies-p m2 (cdr trace) id inc)))))

; The kernel the open hands the machine: L's open kernel (no batch, nothing in flight, every
; committed record acknowledged) that is not faulted.  The open's kernel is one
; (fn-ptx-open-kernelp-of-the-open: fn-lgk-recover leaves :ready), and the relation carries
; "not faulted" through every phase but :fenced (fn-ptx-related-kernel-is-faulted-only-fenced), so
; while unfenced the machine is never related to a faulted kernel.  Without it P4-2 is false: a kernel in
; :fault with no batch is fn-ptd-open-kernelp and R-related to the same store (neither reads the
; phase), fn-ptd-begin refuses its take, and the machine, whose projection fn-ptx-kd-of reads no
; phase, posts.
(defun fn-ptx-open-kernelp (ks)
  (and (fn-ptd-open-kernelp ks) (not (equal (fn-lgk-phase ks) :fault))))

; fn: the POST machine's acceptance spans PRE, INS and SUFTAIL bounded by one
; term K of the sealed configuration (books/post-transaction.lisp
; fn-ptx-acc-end, lane/post, Builder P: |pre|, |ins|, |suftail| <= K).  The
; record's head and tail around the payload are bounded by the records seam
; (books/records-seam.lisp: head <= *fn-record-head-max-octets*, tail <=
; *fn-record-tail-max-octets*); the injected block the head span carries is
; bounded by C's O1 figure (books/filed-stored.lisp
; fn-o1-injected-block-octets, 2236 + 2 len(agent)) at the widest agent a
; configuration admits (books/injection.lisp fn-inj-configp:
; len(agent) <= *fn-inj-max-agent-octets*).  K is derived from those figures,
; never pinned.

(in-package "ACL2")
(include-book "filed-stored")
(include-book "records-seam")

; The injected block at the widest agent a configuration admits.
(defun fn-adm-injected-block-max ()
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (fn-o1-injected-block-octets
   (make-list *fn-inj-max-agent-octets* :initial-element 0)))

; KEYSTONE: an agent within the configuration's bound injects at most
; (fn-adm-injected-block-max) octets.
(defthm fn-adm-injected-block-within-its-max
  (implies (<= (len agent) *fn-inj-max-agent-octets*)
           (<= (fn-o1-injected-block-octets agent) (fn-adm-injected-block-max)))
  :rule-classes :linear
  :hints (("Goal" :in-theory (enable fn-o1-injected-block-octets))))

; Every configuration's own agent meets the hypothesis.
(defthm fn-adm-configured-injected-block-within-its-max
  (implies (fn-inj-configp config)
           (<= (fn-o1-injected-block-octets (fn-inj-config-agent config))
               (fn-adm-injected-block-max)))
  :rule-classes :linear
  :hints (("Goal" :in-theory (e/d (fn-inj-configp) (fn-o1-injected-block-octets
                                                     fn-adm-injected-block-max))
                  :use ((:instance fn-adm-injected-block-within-its-max
                                   (agent (fn-inj-config-agent config)))))))

; K: the widest acceptance span, the record's head with the injected block,
; or its tail.
(defun fn-adm-acc-out-max ()
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (max (+ *fn-record-head-max-octets* (fn-adm-injected-block-max))
       *fn-record-tail-max-octets*))

(defthm fn-adm-acc-out-max-holds-the-head-and-the-block
  (<= (+ *fn-record-head-max-octets* (fn-adm-injected-block-max))
      (fn-adm-acc-out-max))
  :rule-classes nil)

(defthm fn-adm-acc-out-max-holds-the-tail
  (<= *fn-record-tail-max-octets* (fn-adm-acc-out-max))
  :rule-classes nil)

;;; SubmissionTaken (books/owner-results.lisp fn-ores-take-result): WORD
;;; (:idle, :taken, :taken-control, :taken-transit), the submission's ID,
;;; MSGID, stored OCTETS and GROUPS, checked once by the recognizer (the
;;; octet fields are octet lists, the groups lists of them); read here.
(defun fnn-owner-take ()
  ;; fnn-owner-result's check, written out: the entry is dispatched through
  ;; fnn-owner-core, where the host reading (tools/harness_check.py) sees it
  ;; and definterface declares it (lane post-guard-off).
  (let ((value (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-take)))
    (unless (fnn-core 'fn-ores-submission-taken-p value)
      (fnn-fault "owner returned a malformed result from ~(~a~)" 'fn-owner-take))
    value))

(defun fnn-owner-taken-word (taken)
  (fnn-core 'fn-ores-taken-word taken))

(defun fnn-owner-taken-id (taken)
  (fnn-core 'fn-ores-taken-id taken))

(defun fnn-owner-taken-msgid (taken)
  (fnn-octets (fnn-core 'fn-ores-taken-msgid taken)))

(defun fnn-owner-taken-octets (taken)
  (fnn-octets (fnn-core 'fn-ores-taken-octets taken)))

(defun fnn-owner-taken-groups (taken)
  (mapcar #'fnn-octets (fnn-core 'fn-ores-taken-groups taken)))

(defun fnn-owner-refresh-compression (log)
  "The log's compression threshold from the owner's live configuration
(fn-owner-compress-min-octets: the `compress-min-octets' row, 0 off), read
where the batch bounds are read, so a reconfiguration applies from the next
record on (lane compression-extents-2)."
  (when log
    (setf (fnn-log-lz-min log) (fnn-nat (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-compress-min-octets)))))

(defun fnn-owner-publish-prepared (service label)
  "Publish and finish the one ACL2-prepared owner transaction."
  (let* ((store (fnn-owner-service-store service))
         (record (fnn-core-arena-state 'fn-owner-pending-octets))
         ;; The file is named from the staged record's own sequence, ACL2's
         ;; (fn-sbud-pending-sequence); the host keeps no count of its own.
         (sequence (fnn-pending-sequence
                    (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-pending-sequence))))
    (unless (fnn-octet-list-p record)
      (fnn-fault "owner returned malformed ~a transaction" label))
    (when (fnn-store-logp store) (fnn-owner-refresh-compression (fnn-store-log store)))
    (handler-case
        (fnn-publish store sequence (fnn-octets record))
      (fnn-store-indeterminate (e) (error e))
      (fnn-store-fault (e)
        ; A structural/core fault is never a capacity refusal.  Preserve the
        ; fence and propagate it to the service fault boundary.
        (setf (fnn-store-fenced store) t)
        (error e))
      (fnn-store-error (e)
        (unless (fnn-store-fenced store)
          (setf (fnn-store-fenced store) t)
          (unless (eq (fnn-owner-action 'fn-owner-known-abort) :aborted)
            (fnn-indeterminate "owner rejected known ~a abort" label))
          ; A known pre-publication refusal consumed the reservation.  It is
          ; the only error branch that reopens the writer without recovery.
          (setf (fnn-store-fenced store) nil))
        (error e)))
    (setf (fnn-store-fenced store) t)
    (fnn-finish store)
    ;; PRF-252: the committed delta wakes the consumer waits.
    (fnn-owner-signal-commit service)
    :durable))

(defun fnn-owner-preflight-publication (service kind)
  "Ask the owner whether one more record of KIND fits the Store's budget.

ACL2 decides it from the profile it was handed at open and the count of the
Store it carries (host/owner-host.lisp fn-owner-publication-verdict); the
host holds no count and no bound of its own here.  An article is not asked
here: its budget is part of its prepare (fn-owner-prepare)."
  (declare (ignore service))
  (unless (eq (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-publication-verdict kind) :admissible)
    (fnn-refuse "Store transaction budget refuses ~(~a~) transaction" kind))
;;; fn-lb-owner-gate through host/owner-host.lisp fn-owner-login-gate-buffer): under
;;; `posting-policy bound-logins' a bound login's article that is unsigned, or
;;; signed by another principal, is refused with the gate's reason

(defun fnn-owner-attempt-served (service msgid payload groups evidence)

  (fnn-octets-fill payload)
  (let ((gate (fnn-core-buffer-state 'fn-owner-login-gate-buffer)))
    (unless (and (consp gate) (member (first gate) '(:pass :refused)))
      (fnn-fault "owner returned malformed login gate ~a" gate

nn-owner-retention-commit (service event)
  "Publish one ACL2-authored retention event through the normal Store path."
  (unless (and (listp event) (= (length event) 5)
               (member (first event) '(:undertake :release))
               (stringp (second event)) (stringp (third event))
               (stringp (fourth event)) (integerp (fifth event)))
    (fnn-fault "ACL2 returned malformed Store retention event"))
  (let ((store (fnn-owner-service-store service)))
    (fnn-owner-preflight-publication service (first event))
    (let ((*fnn-observe-callback* #'fnn-owner-observe)
                (*fnn-identity-reservation-callback* #'fnn-owner-identity-reservation)
          (*fnn-finish-callback* #'fnn-owner-finish))
      ;; The exact ACL2-authored event funds its reserved release identity
      ;; and produces the one-shot capability consumed by preparation.
      (fnn-advance-frontier store
                            (fnn-nat (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-next-txid)) event)
      (let ((prepared
             (fnn-owner-action
              'fn-owner-prepare-retention (first event)
              (fnn-octet-list (fnn-string-octets (second event)))
              (fnn-octet-list (fnn-string-octets (third event)))
              (fnn-octet-list (fnn-string-octets (fourth event)))
              (fifth event))))
        (unless (eq prepared :prepared)
          (unless (eq (fnn-owner-action 'fn-owner-refuse-reservation) :refused)
            (fnn-indeterminate "owner could not consume refused retention reservation"))
          (fnn-refuse "canonical Store refused retention event")))
      (fnn-owner-publish-prepared service "retention"))))

(defun fnn-owner-identity-commit (service event)
  "Publish one ACL2-constructed keyring snapshot or atomic acceptance event."
  (let ((store (fnn-owner-service-store service)))
    ;; ACL2's verdict over the event itself (host/owner-host.lisp
    ;; fn-owner-identity-publication-verdict): a composite is charged its
    ;; figure, then the memory gate over the row it stages; the refusal
    ;; names the verdict's word (:memory among them).
    (let ((verdict (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-identity-publication-verdict event)))
      (unless (eq verdict :admissible)
        (error 'fnn-identity-verdict-refusal
               :word verdict
               :message (format nil "Store refuses ~(~a~) transaction (~(~a~))"
                                (fnn-core 'fn-wire-event-kind event) verdict))))
    (let ((*fnn-observe-callback* #'fnn-owner-observe)
                (*fnn-identity-reservation-callback* #'fnn-owner-identity-reservation)
          (*fnn-finish-callback* #'fnn-owner-finish))
      (fnn-advance-frontier store
                            (fnn-nat (fnn-owner-core 'fn-owner-next-txid)))
      ;; The entry stages the interned row and reads the arena only; when
      ;; the Store took a composite it names the article's payload and the
      ;; host seals exactly those octets (host/owner-host.lisp
      ;; fn-owner-prepare-identity, books/owner-identity-intern.lisp).
      (let ((prepared (fnn-core-arena-state 'fn-owner-prepare-identity event)))
        (when (and (consp prepared) (eq (first prepared) :seal))
```

# Sonnet brief 9 (Builder P, seat P6): two teeth repairs from the Codex review

Codex reviewed the packet (build/post-runs/codex/codex-findings-post-machine.md; read findings 4 and 6).
Two repairs in tests/acl2/post-transaction-tests.lisp, nothing else in that book changes, and any lemma a
repair needs goes in books/post-transaction-crash.lisp after fn-ptx-p6-pending-is-no-image (additions only;
no existing definition or theorem changes).

1. fn-ptx-closed-is-ok (books/post-transaction.lisp:2037, `(fn-ptx-okp (fn-ptx-closed))`, no hypothesis) has
   NO defteeth in the book (Sonnet 8 reported one; there is none). Add it: claim `(() (fn-ptx-okp (fn-ptx-closed)))`,
   :subject fn-ptx-step (or the function the host calls to make the closed machine, if defteeth demands it),
   an executed positive witness (the book attaches the acceptance, so fn-ptx-okp runs), and one checked
   mutation naming a real fault (e.g. the closed machine built with a queue entry or a nonzero extent:
   a conclusion edit replacing (fn-ptx-closed) with such a state, false of okp). Not a :bogus-phase toy
   unless nothing better exists; say why in the :fault.
2. P6's img removal (ptxt-gl-p6-without-img) uses the store with its write still pending as the image: every
   crash image has no pending writes, so that break tests only the empty-pending field (Codex finding 6).
   Replace it with a semantic break, holding rel, e and sum at the witness's values: an image with NO pending
   writes, octet-valued, equal to a crash image of the unsplit store except one durable byte OUTSIDE the entry's
   footprint (before the frontier, in the committed log, or past frontier + len e) changed. Prove it is an image
   of neither the split store nor the unsplit store, citing a lemma that crash images keep the durable content
   outside every pending write's footprint (prove it for a general store if byte-store has none:
   books/byte-store.lisp fn-bs-crash, fn-bs-crash-select, fn-bs-apply-op; fn-ptx-p6-image-keeps-the-tail is the
   unsplit-store case past the entry and may be reused).

## Binding rules, verbatim (COORDINATION.md)
6e. INCIDENT 2026-10-10 18:32 EDT: a Sonnet of M's ran `pkill -f "cat" -u ember` on the laptop; `-f` matches the whole command line, every app lives under /Applications, and 44 of ember's applications and agents received SIGTERM (Firefox, Terminal, VS Code, Signal, Codex, Tailscale, Little Snitch, the tokeman daemon and more). Rule, sharpened: no seat and no Sonnet runs pkill, killall or kill -u by any pattern, ever; a process of your own is stopped by the PID its PID file holds, nothing else; `pkill -f` with any pattern and `pkill -x` with a bare word are forbidden outright. Every Sonnet brief quotes this rule and the §9 no-kill rule verbatim; a seat whose Sonnet breaks it retires that Sonnet and reports the incident whole, as M did.

§9: No kill, pkill, killall or systemctl stop of anything you did not start, never by name or user match.

## Stopping processes: PID file or nothing (incident 2026-10-10), verbatim from ember's instructions
A subagent ran `pkill -f "cat" -u ember` to clear its own stuck `cat`. `-f` matches the whole
command line, every app lives under `/Appli**cat**ions`, and 44 of my apps and agents got SIGTERM.
- **Never kill by pattern.** No `pkill -f <anything>`, no `pkill -x <bare word>`, no `killall`, no `kill -u`.
- **A process you started is stopped by the PID in its PID file**, and only that. Every detached job
  writes one (`nohup ... & echo $! > job.pid`). No PID file, no kill: tell me instead.
- If you must look first, `pgrep -fl` and read the list; it is never the argument to a kill.
- Every subagent brief you write quotes this block verbatim.

## COORDINATION §9, verbatim
## 9. Rules that came from incidents, kept verbatim in every brief

No kill, pkill, killall or systemctl stop of anything you did not start, never by name or user match. Never any pattern kill (no `pkill -f <anything>`, no `pkill -x <bare word>`, no `killall`, no `kill -u`): a process you started is stopped by the PID in its PID file and only that; with no PID file, tell the coordinator; `pgrep -fl` is for looking only, never the argument to a kill (INCIDENT 2026-10-10 18:32: a Sonnet's `pkill -f "cat" -u ember` sent SIGTERM to 44 of ember's applications). Every Sonnet brief quotes this block verbatim. `timeout` on every long command.   (declare (xargs :mode :program))
  (let* ((ev (get-event f wrld)) (formals (caddr ev)) (rest (cdddr ev))
         (decls (butlast rest 1)) (body (car (last rest))))
    `(defun ,(cdr (assoc-eq f alist)) ,formals
       ,@(fn-rtc-layer-rename decls alist)
       (declare (xargs :normalize nil))
       ,(fn-rtc-layer-rename body alist))))

2026-09-22, planning/review-2026-09-22-proof-engineering.md, F1 and F5).  The
right loop is one ACL2 process with the book's certified dependencies
included from the cache -- seconds -- and the book's own events loaded up to
the one that fails, where each `defthm` attempt costs what the prover spends
on it and nothing else.  This tool is that process, kept alive behind a Unix
socket so that an agent (or a person) can talk to it from any shell.

    python3 tools/proof_repl.py start sni books/store-node-invariants \
        --upto fn-sn-finish-preserves-state
    python3 tools/proof_repl.py send sni '(defthm try1 ... :hints (...))'
    python3 tools/proof_repl.py send sni '(accumulated-persistence t)' --full
    python3 tools/proof_repl.py status sni
```

# Codex worker c1 (Builder P7, POST landing 2): guard verification of the POST machine's step

## Goal
The host will execute the POST machine through its layer's executable copies, so fn-ptx-step must be
guard-verified with guard T (beyond the stobj recognizer fn-rtc-st-p that ACL2 adds), because the layer
generator calls it as the constrained `fn-rtc-mx-step`, whose signature is
`((fn-rtc-mx-step * * fn-rtc-st *) => (mv * * *))` with no guard (books/runtime-contract-exec.lisp:57-68).
Then books/post-transaction-layer.lisp drops its `(set-verify-guards-eagerness 0)` so that
`fn-rtc-def-layer fn-ptl` admits the executable copies fn-ptl-x-* WITH their guards verified (the way
books/runtime-contract-host-lease.lisp's `fn-rtc-def-layer fn-hll` does for its machine).

Real signatures:
- books/post-transaction.lisp:581 `(defun fn-ptx-step (m ev fn-rtc-st q) ...)`, state accessors fn-ptx-* (fn-rtc-get),
  fn-ptx-closed (:151), fn-ptx-opened (:147). The book sets `(set-verify-guards-eagerness 0)` after its includes.
- The constrained acceptance fn-ptx-acc-* (encapsulate at books/post-transaction.lisp ~:57); give the
  encapsulate's signatures guard T only if needed and only by adding `:guard t` style declarations to the
  local witnesses and signatures; NO constraint (defthm inside the encapsulate) changes.
- L's driver functions in books/post-transaction-durable.lisp are L's file: do NOT edit it. If fn-ptx-step
  calls a function whose guards are unverified there, add `(verify-guards <fn>)` events in
  books/post-transaction.lisp (before the first use), with whatever local lemmas they need.

## What you may change
- books/post-transaction.lisp: add `(declare (xargs :guard ...))`, `:guard-hints`, `mbe`/`mbt` ONLY where the
  :logic is the existing body verbatim, `verify-guards` events, and new local lemmas. Every existing defun's
  BODY and every existing defthm/defun-sk/defun-nx's statement stays byte-identical. Prefer guard T on every
  function the step reaches; a non-T guard on a callee is fine when the caller establishes it.
- books/post-transaction-layer.lisp: remove line 20's eagerness setting and the five verify-guards lines if they
  become redundant; nothing else, unless the generator's guard proofs need a hint in the existing :hints form,
  in which case extend only those hints.
- books/post-transaction-sim.lisp, books/post-transaction-crash.lisp, tests/acl2/post-transaction-tests.lisp: only
  if a guard addition breaks one of their proofs; then only hints, never a statement.
- Scratch: build/post-runs/l2/c1/ (logs, notes, your report).

## Gate (exact)
    cd /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post
    timeout 900 python3 tools/proof_repl.py start pc1 books/post-transaction     # iterate here; session names start pc1
    timeout 300 python3 tools/proof_repl.py send pc1 '(verify-guards fn-ptx-step)' --limit 120

It must pass, and in a REPL over books/post-transaction-layer both of these must hold:
    (guard-verified-p 'fn-ptx-step (w state))  =>  T
    (guard-verified-p 'fn-ptl-x-step (w state)) =>  T
Do NOT load books/post-transaction-crash from source after the machine book (rewriter loop): include it certified.

## Do not
skip-proofs, defaxiom, a ttag, `:verify-guards nil` left on anything the step reaches, any change to an existing
theorem statement or definition body, editing any other file, git commit/push/stash/reset/checkout (git is
read-only for you, `git -C /abs/path` only), opening anything outside /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post,
reading personal files or any LANEDUMP.md, launching anything on hbox or persvati, make, any test suite.

verdict and its log path, the two guard-verified-p answers, anything that could not be verified and the exact

                                     word (fn-owner-store-profile state)
                                     (first gate) (second gate) (third gate)
                                     (fn-sbud-count s) bytes record debt tot row)
                                    next))))
                 (state (if (equal record :clock-unusable)
                            state
                          (let ((state (fn-owner-retain-carry-put carry state)))
                            (fn-owner-install-ocfg (cdr outcome) state)))))
            (if (equal record :clock-unusable)
                (mv nil :clock-unusable fn-arena fn-hist state)
              (if (not (equal (car outcome) :prepared))
                (mv nil (car outcome) fn-arena fn-hist state)
              ; The entry reads the arena only (no invariant-risk: it runs
              ; compiled, no callee re-checks its guard); it names the payload
              ; and the host seals it with one fn-arena-seal-list call
              ; (tools/run_owner.py prepare), exactly when ACL2 answered
              ; :prepared.
```

85695 SURVEY.md
# C2 survey for Builder P7 / POST landing 2

Citations and verbatim excerpts are pinned to the requested commits, including the POST proof section edited concurrently during this survey. They are not line numbers for subsequent uncommitted edits. Read trees: post `bf81a299153200406a6926f7bbc8d2836d5f6fb8`; vertical-lease `0666e02e3430e67f4169306b0f284c4b96b128b1`. Unless an absolute vertical-lease path is given, citations are relative to `/Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post`. Source statements are quoted as present in these trees; no ACL2, build, suite, or certification was run. The historical dossier is used only to identify its numbered conversions, then checked against current source.

The key corrections for the packet are: worker completions and delivered events have different field positions (`books/runtime-contract.lisp:298`, `:1401`); there is no `:answer` request in the dispatch (`:1184`); `fn-ptx-message` is in the simulation book (`books/post-transaction-sim.lisp:34`); split ops take lengths, not an offset vector (`books/post-transaction-crash.lisp:7`); and the served buffer prepare still materializes `(fn-octets-list fn-octets)` (`host/owner-host.lisp:2536`).

## 1. Runtime contract v2.2

### Events, actions, requests, and fields

The external event is a completion `(kind id inc op outcome [data])`, admitted by `fn-rtc-completionp` (length 5 or 6); `op` participates in the outstanding-use key. `fn-rtc-e-*` read external completion fields; the machine's delivered event is `(kind outcome id inc . more)` and has no operation field. See `books/runtime-contract.lisp:298`–314 and `:1401`–1407. The `fn-rtc-ev-*` accessors found in this runtime family are `fn-rtc-ev-id`, `fn-rtc-ev-inc`, and `fn-rtc-ev-opened-data`; separate `fn-rtc-ev-kind`, `fn-rtc-ev-outcome`, `fn-rtc-ev-hand-*` definitions were not found. POST reads kind/outcome/more using `fn-rtc-get` (`books/post-transaction.lisp:515`–536).

**Source: `books/runtime-contract.lisp:289`–314. Verbatim:**

```lisp
(defun fn-rtc-outcomep (o)
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (and (true-listp o)
       (case (fn-rtc-get 0 o)
         ((:done :short) (and (equal (len o) 2) (natp (fn-rtc-get 1 o))))
         (:failed (equal (len o) 2))
         (:cancelled (equal (len o) 1))
         (otherwise nil))))

; (kind id inc op outcome) or, for an :in kind, (kind id inc op outcome data).
(defun fn-rtc-completionp (e)
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (and (true-listp e)
       (or (equal (len e) 5) (equal (len e) 6))
       (member-eq (fn-rtc-get 0 e) *fn-rtc-kinds*)
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 1 e))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 2 e))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 3 e))
       (fn-rtc-outcomep (fn-rtc-get 4 e))))

(defun fn-rtc-e-kind (e) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (fn-rtc-get 0 e))
(defun fn-rtc-e-id (e) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 1 e)))
(defun fn-rtc-e-inc (e) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 2 e)))
(defun fn-rtc-e-op (e) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 3 e)))
(defun fn-rtc-e-outcome (e) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (fn-rtc-get 4 e))
(defun fn-rtc-e-data (e) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (fn-rtc-get 5 e))
```

**Source: `books/runtime-contract.lisp:1401`–1407. Verbatim:**

```lisp
; The event an instance receives: (kind outcome id inc . more).
(defun fn-rtc-ev (kind out id inc more)
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (list* kind out id inc more))

(defun fn-rtc-ev-inc (ev) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (fn-rtc-get 3 ev))

Vocabulary, exactly as defined:

- Completion kinds: `(:recv :send :pread :pwrite :fsync :close :accept :timer :bp-send :hand :pool :grant :extend :opened)` (`books/runtime-contract.lisp:120`–121).
- Worker writes buffer / `:in`: `(:recv :pread)`; worker reads buffer / `:out`: `(:send :pwrite :bp-send)` (`:123`–125). `:in` and `:out` are ownership directions, **not submit kinds**.
- Machine submit kinds: `(:recv :send :pread :pwrite :fsync :timer :bp-send :hand :pool :extend)` (`:164`–165). `:accept`, `:grant`, `:opened` are layer-issued; `:close` has its own request (`:162`).
- Action kinds are completion kinds plus `:cancel`; cancellation has no completion of its own (`:167`–169).
- Action layout is `(kind id incarnation op args)` (`:3`–7). Buffered submit emits `(kind id inc op (hd res . extra))`; unbuffered submit emits `(kind id inc op (res . extra))` (`:1121`–1140). `hd = (h g off len)` (`:29`); `res` is the slot resource.
- Ordinary extras are a list of at most two naturals
[… 6542 bytes elided — cv cat agent-a28ab66086a4f8c66 toolu_01J9kxBPvCtVKdBUZeyUYQN5 …]

| T4 | `fn-rtc-outstanding-use-is-stable`: a step not ending U retains U (or grants its wait on `:grant`) and, for a handle, preserves the entire buffer. | `books/runtime-contract.lisp:2294` |

| T7 | `fn-rtc-machine-changes-only-when-delivered-to`: a changed machine J implies acting completion and J equals the runtime target. | `books/runtime-contract-keystones.lisp:260` |
| T8 | `fn-rtc-other-workspaces-are-untouched`: a workspace whose owner slot differs from target remains the same entire buffer. | `books/runtime-contract-keystones.lisp:438` |
| T9 | `fn-rtc-generation-is-monotone`: under invp the buffer generation cannot decrease in a step. | `books/runtime-contract-keystones.lisp:99` |

T8 covers buffers **already owned as workspace**, including ones previously delivered by hand. T4 covers outstanding hands/leases; T16b below pins an in-flight hand's only landing. These are the directly relevant protection statements, rather than a guessed theorem named “another-slot-cannot-write.”

### Static slots, opened, and log write completion

Slot 0 is listener. Slots 1..nstatic are live at incarnation 1 from init and cannot be closed; owner is slot 1 (`books/runtime-contract.lisp:17`–23, `:210`–214, `:1044`–1055). `fn-rtc-init` builds listener/static/free slots, free pool, static machine initial states and empty uses, arms one `:opened` for every static slot, then rearms accept/grant (`:1601`–1645). Each opened action is `(:opened j 1 op (0))` (`:1615`–1622). External completion `(:opened j 1 op (:done 0) (KD EXT GEO))` becomes machine `(:opened (:done 0) j 1 (KD EXT GEO))`; failure/cancellation delivers `(:opened OUT j 1)` without data. Use consumption permits at most one delivery; machine cannot submit/cancel opened (`:146`–160; `books/runtime-contract-opened.lisp:45`, `:338`, `:541`).

POST uses log file ref `*fn-ptx-lfile* = 2` (`books/post-transaction.lisp:31`). Its write request is `(:submit :pwrite (h g off n) (2 pos))` (`:312`). Runtime emits `(:pwrite id inc op (hd res 2 pos))` by the buffered submit arm (`books/runtime-contract.lisp:1138`–1140). A host completion is `(:pwrite id inc op (:done n))` (or short/failed/cancelled); runtime matches use, ends the lease, validates count against handle, and delivers `(:pwrite OUT id inc)` to that machine (`:871`–890, `:1476`–1479, `:1544`–1550). POST requires `:done` and exactly the span length in both write phases (`books/post-transaction.lisp:556`–567). A successful pwrite itself is **not** POST commit: final log `:fsync` tagged done is (`:568`–573). This is the model/executor delivery protocol; the current synchronous host log path in §5 does not drive ptx with these events.

## 2. POST transaction machine

`fn-ptx-step` returns `(mv m2 requests cost)`; failed bounded shape check gives `(mv (fn-ptx-broken) nil (fn-ptx-wf-cost))` (`books/post-transaction.lisp:580`–586). `fn-ptx-spans` returns a list of `(h off n)` triples, empty spans omitted, in order: b1 prefix; article before Path insertion; b1 insertion; article after insertion; b3 suffix/trailer (`:250`–264). `fn-ptx-msg-range` returns an octet list of n octets from these logical concatenated spans, via view byte reads (`:268`–281). `fn-ptx-message` returns the entire spans' concatenation via msg-range; it is **not found as a defun in post-transaction.lisp**, but is defined in simulation (`books/post-transaction-sim.lisp:34`–37). `fn-ptx-cur-code` observes the current post's code from the emitted answer pair; it is not a host notification primitive (`books/post-transaction.lisp:695`–709).

**Source: `books/post-transaction.lisp:311`–326. Verbatim:**

```lisp
(defun fn-ptx-timer () (list :submit :timer nil (list 0)))
(defun fn-ptx-pwrite (h g off n pos) (list :submit :pwrite (list (nfix h) (nfix g) (nfix off) (nfix n)) (list *fn-ptx-lfile* (nfix pos))))
(defun fn-ptx-fsync () (list :submit :fsync nil (list *fn-ptx-lfile*)))
; fn-ptd's extension as the unbuffered :extend kind (contract v2.2): FILE's extent
; from EXT to NXT; the layer accepts it only with naturals below 2^64 and EXT < NXT.
(defun fn-ptx-extend (ext nxt) (list :submit :extend nil (list *fn-ptx-lfile* (nfix ext) (nfix nxt))))

; The answer O to post P = (fid finc bp len ...): octet 0 of the poster's own
; buffer becomes the code, the whole buffer goes back by a hand.  The generation
; is the view's now (a lease's end advances it).
(defun fn-ptx-answer (p o id inc fn-rtc-st)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st))
  (let* ((h (fn-ptx-cur-bp p)) (g (fn-ptx-gen h id inc fn-rtc-st))
         (fl (max 1 (nfix (fn-rtc-st-v-fill h id inc fn-rtc-st)))))
    (list (list :write h g 0 (list (fn-ptx-code o)))
          (list :submit :hand (list h g 0 fl) (list (fn-ptx-cur-fid p) (fn-ptx-cur-finc p))))))
```

(defun fn-ptx-span-pwrite (i m front id inc fn-rtc-st)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st))
  (let* ((spans (fn-ptx-spans m)) (sp (fn-rtc-get i spans)) (h (nfix (fn-rtc-get 0 sp))))
    (fn-ptx-pwrite h (fn-ptx-gen h id inc fn-rtc-st) (fn-rtc-get 1 sp) (fn-rtc-get 2 sp)
                   (+ (nfix front) (fn-ptx-spans-len (take (nfix i) spans))))))
```

Requests **do carry inline octets** in `:write`: nine zeros, acceptance PRE/INS/suffix parts, trailer, one-byte answer. `fn-rtc-reqs-octets` counts only `(len field-4)` of `:write` requests; pwrite uses handles, with no inline article octets (`books/runtime-contract.lisp:529`–536; POST exact expressions `books/post-transaction.lisp:424`–426, `:463`–465, `:498`, `:325`). Step-facts bounds cost plus these inline octets by q+C; it does not assert zero request octets. The article remains in the poster's transferred buffer and is read/written through spans (`:250`–264).

## 5. Current synchronous POST: socket → durable record

### Actual call chain

1. `fnn-mux-step (loop conn)` dispatches its incoming bytes to `fnn-owner-handle-chunk-step` with service/cid/socket/class/peerp and cold/clock continuation values (`host/native/mux.lisp:967`, `:983`–994). The warm arm of handle-chunk-step calls `fnn-owner-handle-chunk-read` (`host/native/owner.lisp:6905`–6918).
2. `fnn-owner-handle-chunk-read` runs under owner serialization, fills the read buffer, captures reader context, and calls `fnn-owner-chunk-span-no-io` (`host/native/owner.lisp:7016`–7040, `:7096`–7105). That wrapper calls `fn-owner-chunk-span` (`:6268`–6274), which delegates to `fn-owner-chunk-span-at` (`host/owner-host.lisp:5455`–5457). The served framing uses spans for the incoming socket chunk; article accumulation still uses body chunks (`host/native/owner.lisp:7088`–7095; `books/wire.lisp:770`–787).
3. At terminator, `fn-wire-after-line` produces `(:article line-list)` from packed body (`books/wire.lisp:785`–787). `fn-nntp-post-step` sees :article while awaiting POST, calls `fn-post-body-octets`, then `fn-post-gated-decision`; successful injection yields submission and **no acceptance reply yet** (`books/nntp-post.lisp:464`–490). `fn-own-enqueue` packs the submission (`books/owner.lisp:1287`).
4. Inline synchronous path: submitted flag calls `fnn-owner-drain-one` with synchronous feed-flush callbacks (`host/native/owner.lisp:7185`–7196). Drain takes a submission, obtains article vector, publishes durable feed intent, calls `fnn-owner-attempt-served` for ordinary served POST, publishes resolution, invokes `fn-owner-outcome`, and returns cid/output/uncertainty/word (`:4371`–4387, `:4433`–4488). The batched path also calls drain from `fnn-owner-commit-start-locked` (`:4569`, `:4612`–4615); §5's inline chain should not be mistaken for the only production scheduling path.
5. `fnn-owner-attempt-served` fills fn-octets from article vector once, invokes login gate, then `fnn-owner-attempt-filled`, and maps the result through `fn-owner-served-post-word` (`host/native/owner.lisp:3991`–4006).
6. `fnn-owner-attempt-filled` first applies control-filing-buffer to groups, then peer-carrier-form-buffer. Carrier absent delegates to `fnn-owner-attempt`; malformed carrier is refused; present carrier goes through carrier plan and signature observations / kind-4 identity commit (`:3670`–3740, `:3784`–3842). These branches do not all call the generic article prepare.
7. Ordinary `fnn-owner-attempt` resolves group codes, charge and post boundary, tests existing article through the buffer, advances/reserves frontier, derives subject/obligation from buffer, calls **fn-owner-prepare-buffer**, checks catalog seal gate, seals once, calls fn-owner-cat-prepare-sealed, and then fnn-owner-publish-prepared (`:3492`–3509, `:3529`–3584).
8. `fnn-owner-publish-prepared`: fetch encoded staged record via fn-owner-pending-octets, check octet list, list→vector, `fnn-publish`, then `fnn-finish`, wake commit consumers, return :durable (`:3377`–3409).
9. `fnn-publish` requires record-log store and calls `fnn-log-publish` (`host/native/io.lisp:4274`–4284). Log publish checks profile publication, compresses if requested, checks frame verdict, fences store, takes record into kernel batch, and when not under batch mode calls `fnn-log-commit-open-batch`; observes log-order, marks completion pending, reaches :record-completing cut and returns :durable (`:9602`–9627).
10. Synchronous commit-open-batch awaits prior sync, ensures extent, appends, cuts :log-written, fences, cuts :log-fenced; write/barrier OS failures fence kernel and raise indeterminate (`:9506`–9542). `fnn-log-append` obtains ACL2 frontier/append-octets and positioned-writes it (`:8059`–8087). `fnn-log-fence` makes name durable, fdatasyncs descriptor, then calls fn-lgc-fence under kernel lock (`:8258`–8288). A publication under batch mode stages completion until the batch barrier; its finish/ack is `fnn-log-batch-finish` (`:9636`–9642), not a pwrite completion into fn-ptx-step.

### What is checked where, and what needs the body

“fn-owner-prepare checks login/signature” is too broad: the login and present-carrier signature decisions occur **before** prepare in the native attempt, while prepare builds/validates/stages the ordinary held row. The following table describes the current composed path and distinguishes data dependence from what the current implementation happens to scan.

| Decision / construction | Actual function / site | Head versus body dependence |
|---|---|---|
| Syntax / size / header envelope at POST terminator | fn-inj-decide checks source bound before fn-article-parse, then census/proto check, mandatory fields, allowed groups, final injected size (`books/injection.lisp:966`–1048). | Header decisions are on fields; total size includes body length. Current parser **also validates CRLF through entire body** at separator, so this stage is not presently a head-only walk (`books/article.lisp:325`–333, `:357`–367). |
| Login binding | fn-owner-login-gate-buffer → fn-ars-lb-ocfg-gate → fn-lb-owner-gate, tests whether carrier exists and claimed principal equals login binding (`host/owner-host.lisp:4602`–4608; `books/article-buffer.lisp:953`–957; `books/login-binding.lisp:67`–81). | Semantic comparison uses carrier/header identity and recorded login, not content digest/body signature. It does not verify signature. Full buffer is input; no assertion that its underlying carrier parser is currently head-only. |
| Control filing | fn-owner-control-filing-buffer before carrier check (`host/native/owner.lisp:3693`–3705). | Control header/groups decision. Ordinary groups unchanged; control article's groups become control.<verb> or refusal (`:3681`–3684). |
| Carrier classification / signature | fn-owner-peer-carrier-form-buffer, then fn-owner-peer-carrier-plan on cached payload list; fn-hsig-host-preimage principal keys source, native observations, require both verified (`host/native/owner.lisp:3706`–3740, `:3786`–3810). | Form identity is header data; cryptographic subject is plan's source article, so signature acceptance must not be replaced by checking only the head. Present carrier takes list/composite path; absence takes ordinary buffer path. |
| Message-ID/existing source identity | fn-owner-existing-action-buffer in ordinary attempt; fn-pidx-existing-action-cat inside prepare-buffer (`host/native/owner.lisp:3529`–3532; `host/owner-host.lisp:2523`–2524). | D25 comparison is source octets through injection inverse, not just Message-ID/head; entire article content matters (`books/injection.lisp:805`–810; `host/native/owner.lisp:3424`–3427). |
| Obligation / content identity | fnn-metadata-buffer → fnn-subject-id-buffer / fn-sidb-subject-id-bounded, then obligation id (`host/native/io.lisp:2123`–2136, `:4356`–4368). | Whole payload digest, read by buffer index; short fixed preimage head, no full payload list for this digest. |
| Input domain | fn-pfld-article-inputsp: Message-ID grammar, payload length, nonempty groups, metadata text, positive charge (`books/post-fields.lisp:90`–100), plus list entry fn-octet-listp (`host/owner-host.lisp:2294`–2297). | Grammar/head metadata and payload length, not body bytes. The list entry's octet-domain check does walk payload. |
| Record / held row construction | fn-sn-article-record; fn-apc-intern-row-at with parse carry (`host/owner-host.lisp:2313`–2319, `:2351`–2354; buffer materialization `:2534`–2540; `books/owner-parse-carried.lisp:999`–1011). | Stores whole article. fn-apc-held-facts-of computes byte count, split, body lines, control/overview facts; body-line count is whole-body data (`books/owner-parse-carried.lisp:980`–986). Held context is at keyring generation; no general claim this is merely header acceptance. |
| Group selection/membership/numbers | code→distinct groups; fn-psrv-event-servedp, fn-psrv-event-numberedp; fn-ppc-accept-prepare group selection + fn-allocate-memberships (`books/store-config.lisp:92`; `books/owner-prepare-served.lisp:29`–63; `books/post-prepare-catalog.lisp:52`–79). | Group names, live configuration/watermarks, not body octets. Membership is locally allocated, not supplied from article body. |
| Transaction/history/maintenance budget | fn-pvc-article-budget-carried then fn-pout-prepare-article / fn-prc-sbud-prepare; budget zero if refused (`host/owner-host.lisp:2341`–2345, `:2363`–2387; buffer :2549 onward). | Payload length, group count, existing bytes/count/debt and record metadata, not the byte values in body. |
| Memory gate | fn-adm-memory-admitp over carried totals plus exact row; if false budget=0; fn-adm-article-word names T, H, then memory (`host/owner-host.lisp:2362`–2387; `books/admission-memory.lisp:39`–68). | Depends on r

rofile)` returns scalar bytes, and charges record/header lists plus unpacked taken submission lists in service; the two retained octet buffers are excluded from this serving term. ## Packet-use gaps and distinctions

- `fn-ptx-message` belongs to the simulation book, and `fn-ptx-split-ops*` was not found (`books/post-transaction-sim.lisp:34`; `books/post-transaction-crash.lisp:7`).
- Runtime request refusals are output-list entries with no completion; POST outcome :refused is the machine's one-byte reply code, a different level (`books/runtime-contract.lisp:1108`–1128; `books/post-transaction.lisp:36`, `:325`).
- Poster article hand producer / concrete poster machine slot is not defined by post-transaction.lisp. This book only consumes sender fields of delivered :handed; owner is static slot 1 (`books/post-transaction.lisp:11`, `:533`–536).
- ptd fourth-value host ops, ptd syscall actions, RTC handle actions, byte-store pending ops, and synchronous host calls are five different surfaces. Their bridges need named composition statements; do not substitute one signature for another (§1–§5 citations).
- Source theorem presence is not current-closure certification. The POST book's header still warns its keystones are packet statements/not certification of record (`books/post-transaction.lisp:7`–9); this survey makes no certification claim.
- The dossier eight are reproducible numbered boundaries, not an allocation census. Current served prepare's payload-list construction and log verdict/take/compression lists are additional current sites (`host/owner-host.lisp:2536`; `host/native/io.lisp:7731`, `:9425`, `:9438`).

(defun fn-ptx-head-len () (+ (len (fn-ptx-frame-header 0)) 32))
(defun fn-ptx-message (m id inc fn-rtc-st)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st))
  (let ((spans (fn-ptx-spans m)))
    (fn-ptx-msg-range 0 (fn-ptx-spans-len spans) spans id inc fn-rtc-st)))
; The record: the message (the trailer included once hashed) less the head and the trailer.
(defun fn-ptx-record (m id inc fn-rtc-st)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st))
  (let ((w (fn-ptx-message m id inc fn-rtc-st)))
    (nthcdr (fn-ptx-head-len) (take (- (len w) 32) w))))

; The digest's held stack over the whole windows of PREFIX (fn-b3-append-window's state).
(defun fn-ptx-stack-of (prefix)
  (if (consp prefix)
      (fn-b3-stack-push-all *fn-b3-iv* 0 *fn-ptx-wexp*
                            (fn-b3-window-outs *fn-b3-iv* *fn-ptx-wexp* 0 prefix 0) nil)
    nil))

; While hashing: the message is octets, holds its whole head, which is the frame header of its
; length and the chain field, and the held stack is the digest's state over the octets absorbed.
(defun fn-ptx-hash-invp (m id inc fn-rtc-st)
  (declare (xargs :stobjs fn-rtc-st))
  (let ((w (fn-ptx-message m id inc fn-rtc-st)) (hs (fn-ptx-hs m)))
    (and (fn-cbor-octet-listp w)
         (<= (fn-ptx-head-len) (len w))
         (equal (take (fn-ptx-head-len) w)
                (append (fn-ptx-frame-header (- (len w) (fn-ptx-head-len))) (fn-ptx-kd-last (fn-ptx-kd m))))
         (equal (fn-rtc-get 1 hs) (fn-ptx-stack-of (take (nfix (fn-rtc-get 0 hs)) w))))))

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; The map: the fn-ptd inputs one machine step gives.  R is the record read after the step; FO the
; host's outcome of a failed log action (any value but :ok; the trace supplies it).
(defun fn-ptx-ptd-ins (m ev m2 r fo)
  (let ((ph (fn-ptx-ph m)) (kind (fn-rtc-get 0 ev)) (ph2 (fn-ptx-ph m2))
        (bad (if (equal fo :ok) :eio fo)))
    (cond ((and (eq ph :hashing) (member-eq ph2 '(:extending :payload-writing))) (list (list :begin r)))
          ((and (eq ph :extending) (eq kind :extend))
           (list (cons :pwrite (if (eq ph2 :extsyncing) :ok bad))))
          ((and (eq ph :extsyncing) (eq kind :fsync))
           (list (cons :fsync (if (eq ph2 :payload-writing) :ok bad))))
          ((and (eq ph :payload-writing) (eq kind :pwrite))
           (if (eq ph2 :fenced) (list (cons :pwrite bad)) nil))
          ((and (eq ph :log-writing) (eq kind :pwrite))
           (list (cons :pwrite (if (eq ph2 :committing) :ok bad))))
          ((and (eq ph :committing) (eq kind :fsync))
           (list (cons :fsync (if (fn-ptx-committedp m2) :ok bad))))
          (t nil))))

; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; The relation.  Phases map; at rest the kernel projection is the driver kernel's, which has no
; batch, nothing in flight and no fault (fn-ptd-begin's take and append need all three) and has
; acknowledged every committed record (the commit's acknowledgement counts one), and while
; hashing the digest's state is the message's; in flight the projection is the kernel's before the
; take (one txid less), the extents agree, the driver's record is the machine's, the message
; with the padding is the driver's entry, and the kernel holds the record as the driver's
; invariant fn-ptd-invp has it: before the seal the batch is the record, nothing is in flight and
; the append admits it (so the kernel is not faulted); after the seal the record is in flight
; and the kernel is :appended; and the trailer the machine holds is the chain's trailer of the
; record (the commit makes it the chain head).  (Restated from Sonnet 6's counterexample: a held
; trailer other than the digest is otherwise related, and the commit then diverges from the fence.)  (Restated from the counterexample of a driver at :writing over a
; kernel with an empty batch: its two :ok completions answer :posted and commit nothing, while
; the machine commits the record.)
```

offset fn-pacc-splice answers.\n(defthm fn-pacc-stored-payload-is-one-splice\n  (implies (and (fn-octet-listp art) (fn-pacc-injectionp inj art))\n           (mv-let (p0 ins ioff) (fn-pacc-splice art inj)\n             (and (natp ioff) (<= ioff (len art))\n                  (equal (fn-pacc-stored-payload art inj)\n                         (append p0 (take ioff art) ins (nthcdr ioff art)))))))\n; The rely's discharge for layer runs (P4's open condition).  The owner's trace of a product run:\n;    every event delivered to slot 1 at incarnation 1, with the view-state of slot 1 before it and the\n;    host outcome FO (:ok when the completion's outcome is (:done n) with n the request's count, else the\n;    outcome itself).\n(defun-nx fn-pth-owner-trace (s events q) ...)   ; (ev view q fo) per delivered slot-1 event, in order\n; R0 (the joint invariant's consequence, Codex 2's point): from a reachable product state the layer\n; refuses none of the owner machine's requests, so no refused write leaves the machine ahead of its buffers.\n(defthm fn-pth-owner-requests-are-never-refused\n  (implies (and (fn-pth-reachablep s) (equal (fn-rtc-e-id e) 1))\n           (not (mv-nth 2 (fn-pth-step* s e q)))))\n; KEYSTONE R1 fn-pth-runs-keep-the-rely: every product run from a reachable state keeps the rely along\n; the owner's trace.\n(defthm fn-pth-runs-keep-the-rely\n  (implies (fn-pth-reachablep s)\n           (fn-ptx-trace-relies-p (fn-pth-mstate 1 s) (fn-pth-owner-trace s events q) 1 1)))\n; fn-pth-reachablep: s = (fn-pth-run (mv-nth 0 (fn-pth-init cfg)) events0 q0) for a fn-rtc-configp cfg\n; whose host delivers completions per A-HOST-LANDS (books/assumptions-runtime.lisp).\n;\n; =====================================================================================================\n; E. (defun fn-rtc-set (i v l)
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (if (consp l)
      (if (zp (nfix i)) (cons v (cdr l)) (cons (car l) (fn-rtc-set (- (nfix i) 1) v (cdr l))))
    nil))
; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; -----------------------------------------------------------------------------
; Configuration (nslots nbufs capf nstatic qfixed npool capl): instance slots
; including the listener, buffers, the capacity of a home buffer in octets,
; the static instances (slots 1..nstatic, live at incarnation 1 from the
; initial state and never closed; the owner is slot 1, so an :accept never
; binds them), the home buffers per instance, the large buffers, and the
; capacity of a large buffer.  Buffers [0, (nslots-1)*qfixed) are home
; buffers: buffer h's home is instance slot 1 + floor(h / qfixed), and an
; instance acquires only free buffers of its own home, so what one instance
; can acquire never depends on what another holds.  Buffers
; [(nslots-1)*qfixed, nbufs) are the pool of npool large buffers, held one
; per large holder and granted in request order (`fn-rtc-grant').  The
; host sizes qfixed and npool from the profile by Builder M's
; books/memory-model.lisp (lane/memory): `fn-mm-instance-qfixed' (with
; `fn-mm-instance-qfixed-is-the-ceiling') and `fn-mm-large-pool' (with
; `fn-mm-large-pool-within-the-leases'); the connection term is
; C*qfixed*capf + npool*capl (`fn-mm-connection-fixed').
(defun fn-rtc-configp (c)
  (declare (xargs :guard t))
  (and (true-listp c) (equal (len c) 7)
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 0 c)) (<= 2 (fn-rtc-get 0 c))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 1 c))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 2 c))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 3 c)) (< (fn-rtc-get 3 c) (fn-rtc-get 0 c))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 4 c))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 5 c))
       (natp (fn-rtc-get 6 c))
       (equal (fn-rtc-get 1 c) (+ (* (- (fn-rtc-get 0 c) 1) (fn-rtc-get 4 c)) (fn-rtc-get 5 c)))))
(defun fn-rtc-nslots (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 0 c)))
(defun fn-rtc-nbufs (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 1 c)))
(defun fn-rtc-capf (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 2 c)))
(defun fn-rtc-nstatic (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 3 c)))
(defun fn-rtc-qfixed (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 4 c)))
(defun fn-rtc-npool (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 5 c)))
(defun fn-rtc-capl (c) (declare (xargs :guard t)) (nfix (fn-rtc-get 6 c)))

**agent** (10-12 09:39):

The pool-buffer question M21 raised as a RED risk to the memory equation does not arise. Nothing is proved or built yet; Codex workers c1 (guard verification of `fn-ptx-step`) and c3 (the context format's definitions) are still running.

**What M21 asked.** Item 4 of its audit asked whether the buffer size CAPL is set per connection. If it were, raising it to hold a post's context plus the largest article would multiply the per-connection charge by the connection count.

**What the code says.** CAPL sizes the shared pool of large buffers, not each connection's buffers:
- `fn-hl-rtc-config` (`books/runtime-contract-host-pool.lisp:30`) creates `npool = (fn-mm-cfg-holders cfg)` large buffers, each of capacity `(fn-hl-article-capl profile q)`.
- Per-connection home buffers stay C·qfixed·capf.
- M's large-pool term is already npool·capl (`fn-mm-large-pool` = holders × large-reply).

So the poster's pool size P equals npool, and posters share those buffers with the leased readers. Per-connection terms do not change.

**The raise.** CAPL becomes `max(fn-hl-article-capl, fn-pctx-max + A)`, where A is the profile's largest article. The context maximum is 1 + 8 + 5·4 + 3·2782 + 9 = 8,384 octets. `fn-hl-article-capl` is at most 2A plus the reply status. That bound already leaves room for A + 8,384 once A is at least 8,384, so the raise only moves small profiles, by at most 8,384 octets per pool buffer.

**Correction to send M21.** My reply to M21 gave the threshold as 8,383, one octet short; the correct figure is 8,384, as stated in the packet. I'll send M21 the correction.

**Packet change.** I added section B to `/Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post/build/post-runs/l2/STATEMENTS.lsp`, with two keystones for M to restate the pool term over:
- **B1 `fn-pth-capl-bound`:** CAPL is at most `max(2A + status, A + fn-pctx-max)`.
- **B2 `fn-pth-capl-holds-a-post`:** one pool buffer holds a post's context and the largest article.

M21 has accepted items 1, 2, 3 and 5 of its audit.

Next, once the workers finish: audit c1's diff for byte-identical statements, then check c3's `fn-pctx-p` against the packet. c3 may have started from the version before M's bounds were folded in. After that, run the hbox counterexample searches and brief the proof workers and the host realizer `host/native/post.lisp`.

Each carries from the dispatch lemma: the combined step at any slot other than 1 is A's reader step. The quantified ones also need the machine-state check split per slot: slot 1 within the POST machine's maximum, every other slot within the reader's. A's statements stay unchanged.

▶ **Bash** `toolu_01HyLoK8ktd9Yzdfuz6zTgA5` — Audit c1 diff: statements and bodies
```
mkdir -p /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post/build/post-runs/l2/audit; cat > /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post/build/post-runs/l2/audit/formdiff.py <<'EOF'
"""Compare top-level forms of two ACL2 files: theorem statements byte-identical (normalized whitespace),
defun bodies identical modulo (mbe :logic X :exec Y) -> X and dropped declare forms."""
import sys, re, subprocess
def toks(s):
    i=0; n=len(s); out=[]
    while i<n:
        c=s[i]
        if c==';':
            while i<n and s[i]!='\n': i+=1
        elif s.startswith('#|',i): i=s.index('|#',i)+2
        elif c.isspace(): i+=1
        elif c in '()': out.append(c); i+=1
        elif c=="'" : out.append("'"); i+=1
        elif c=='`' or c==',': 
            if s.startswith(',@',i): out.append(',@'); i+=2
            else: out.append(c); i+=1
        elif c=='"':
            j=i+1
            while s[j]!='"': j+= 2 if s[j]=='\\' else 1
            out.append(s[i:j+1]); i=j+1
        elif s.startswith('#\\',i):
            j=i+3
            while j<n and not s[j].isspace() and s[j] not in '()': j+=1
            out.append(s[i:j]); i=j
        else:
            j=i
            while j<n and not s[j].isspace() and s[j] not in '()\'"`,;': j+=1
            if s[j-1:j]=='|': pass
            out.append(s[i:j].lower()); i=j
    return out
def parse(t,i):
    if t[i]=='(':
        l=[]; i+=1
        while t[i]!=')':
            x,i=parse(t,i); l.append(x)
        return l,i+1
    if t[i] in ("'",'`',',',',@'):
        x,i=parse(t,i+1); return [t[i-0] if False else 'quote:'+t[i-1] if False else 'q', x],i
    return t[i],i+1
def forms(s):
    t=toks(s); i=0; out=[]
    while i<len(t):
        x,i=parse(t,i); out.append(x)
    return out
def strip(x):
    if isinstance(x,list):
        if len(x)==5 and x[0]=='mbe' and x[1]==':logic': return strip(x[2])
        if len(x)==5 and x[0]=='mbe' and x[3]==':logic': return strip(x[4])
        return [strip(y) for y in x if not (isinstance(y,list) and y and y[0]=='declare')]
    return x
def key(f):
    if isinstance(f,list) and len(f)>1 and isinstance(f[1],str): return (f[0],f[1])
    return None
def index(fs):
    d={}
    for f in fs:
        k=key(f)
        if k: d.setdefault(k,f)
    return d
base=index(forms(open(sys.argv[1]).read())); head=index(forms(open(sys.argv[2]).read()))
thm={'defthm','defthmd','defun-sk','defun-nx','defund-nx','defaxiom','defchoose','defconst','encapsulate'}
bad=0
for k,f in base.items():
    if k not in head: print('MISSING',k); bad+=1; continue
    g=head[k]
    if k[0] in ('defun','defund'):
        if strip(f)!=strip(g): print('BODY-CHANGED',k); bad+=1
    else:
        # statements: compare whole form minus :hints/:rule-classes? compare statement position only
        if k[0] in ('defthm','defthmd'):
            if f[2]!=g[2]: print('STATEMENT-CHANGED',k); bad+=1
            rc=lambda h: [h[i+1] for i in range(3,len(h)-1) if h[i]==':rule-classes']
            if rc(f)!=rc(g): print('RULE-CLASSES-CHANGED',k); bad+=1
        elif strip(f)!=strip(g): print('FORM-CHANGED',k); bad+=1
new=[k for k in head if k not in base]
print('base forms',len(base),'head forms',len(head),'new',len(new),'bad',bad)
for k in new: print('NEW',k)
EOF
cd /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post; for b in post-transaction post-transaction-layer; do git -C /Users/ember/dev/fn/build/lanes/post show HEAD:books/$b.lisp > build/post-runs/l2/audit/$b.base.lisp; echo "== $b"; python3 build/post-runs/l2/audit/formdiff.py build/post-runs/l2/audit/$b.base.lisp books/$b.lisp; done 2>&1 | tail -40

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Run one ACL2 `test?` counterexample search and say what it actually did.
`test?` prints "Test? succeeded. No counterexamples were found." when it never
tested anything: for a conjecture over a function that takes a stobj (or a
constrained function without an attachment) Cgen skips testing completely and
says so only in its verbose output (P6 on hbox, 2026-10-11: fn-ptx-step and
the rest).  It also reports "proved" for a conjecture whose hypotheses no
generated example satisfied.  Most of fn's keystones are over stobjs, so the
final "Test?" line is not evidence of a search (COUNTEREXAMPLE rule 6.6).
This runs `test?` at verbosity level 3, reads Cgen's own summary, and prints
exactly one line:
    SEARCHED <m> examples 0 counterexamples (<n> tested, <u> unique)   exit 0
    COUNTEREXAMPLE <first binding>                                     exit 1
    SKIPPED <reason>                                                   exit 2
It never prints SEARCHED unless Cgen's summary says at least one example
satisfied the hypotheses and none falsified the conclusion.  The full verbose
transcript is written to --log (default: a fresh file under
build/test-search/) and its path goes to stderr.
    python3 tools/test_search.py --book books/heap-figure \\
        '(implies (natp x) (<= 0 (fnn-figure x)))'
ACL2 is `$FN_ACL2`, defaulting on a farm box to that box's launcher
(`acl2_slots.apply_box_defaults`), and runs under the tree's slot pool with the
certification environment.  acl2s/cgen must be certified for that launcher
(hbox: planning/hbox-acl2s-cgen-2026-10-11.md).
"""
from __future__ import annotations
import argparse
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import subprocess
import sys

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Run one ACL2 under the machine-wide slot pool, for interactive `ld` work.

`tools/certify_books.py` takes a slot from `tools/acl2_slots.py` before every
ACL2 it starts, so the runner never exceeds the pool.  A lane iterating on a
scratch driver does not go through the runner: it types `acl2 < driver.lsp`,
which takes no slot at all.  On 2026-09-19 that put six ACL2 processes on a
laptop whose pool is four, and the box swapped.

This wrapper is the `ld` path's entry to the same pool.  It acquires exactly
one slot the way the runner does -- the same lock directory, the same
once-a-minute wait line on stderr, so a wait is never mistaken for a hang --
sets the same two environment variables the runner sets, and runs `$FN_ACL2`
(default: the launcher ~/.config/fn/acl2 names, else `acl2`;
`acl2_slots.configured_acl2`) with this process's stdin, stdout and
```

**agent** (10-12 10:05):

c4's run of the acceptance definitions came back with a RED, but I traced it to my own brief, not to the machine's constraint. I've resumed c4 with a fix that leaves the constraint untouched. Nothing is proved yet: searches come first.

**What c4 found.** In `books/post-acceptance.lisp`, six of the seven acceptance constraints prove with their bodies unchanged. The seventh, feed-bounded, fails: it requires every feed result's size to stay within `fn-pacc-max-state` for any input state. My brief had the feed return its input state unchanged, so an oversized state stays oversized. c4's counterexample is 16,727 zero octets fed in, giving a state of size 33,455.

**The fix.** The feed and the end now pass their state through a normalizer. It keeps a state only if it has the shape `fn-pacc-begin` produces, and otherwise turns it into nil, which ends as a refusal. The machine only ever feeds the state that begin produced, so behaviour on reachable states doesn't change. c4 re-runs the ground file and the certify, and must prove the feed-bounded constraint with its body unchanged.

**Evidence so far** (one real two-group post, `build/post-runs/l2/c4/ground.out`):
- **A0, the stored payload as one splice:** equal to the batch path's stored payload. The injection is one Path insert after a prefix made of the Cancel-Lock lines and the injected header block, matching the layout ruling (a) assumed.
- **A1 and A3, the laid-out record and the batch path's published octets:** the comparison as stated can't be evaluated, because the record codec is constrained and not attached in this book. The same comparisons against the concrete encoder `fn-record-encode-impl` are both equal at every index.
- **The batch path's composition:** `fn-pacc-batch-record-octets` is non-executable (a `defun-nx` over the arena), so A3's teeth will need a real prepared post run through the image.

**Waiting:**
- c4's fix, behind a background wait.
- The hbox counterexample searches for the context-format keystones C1–C3, which start when hbox's load drops below 20.

Committed on lane/post: 9ddd948d4, the guard verification, and 7bc3f3d6b, a comment fix.
